Performance-improvement review corpus

**Current scope freeze:** [485-PR index and policy](mining/README.md), [exposure ledger](mining/evaluation/exposure.json), and [evaluation protocol](mining/evaluation/protocol.json). Historical sections below are preserved; previous holdout labels do not establish freshness. Real-source availability gaps block evaluation acceptance, not inventory accounting.

Snapshot and scope

This is an acquisition record, not a reviewer, evidence-derived rules, or a review checklist. It preserves candidate selection, source provenance, historical revisions and an untouched family-level evaluation reserve.

The original inventory/split tables below are the pre-drafting snapshot. Current reconciled status is in final source-coverage accounting and effective applicability coverage; acquisition history remains in the reserved-source study. Historical "untouched" and "not evaluated" statements describe that earlier freeze, not the later execution status. Source-attribution corrections are in the named conversation audit; no original-user authorship attestation is claimed.

Field

Frozen value

Repository

dotnet/fsharp

Calendar window

2026-04-22 through 2026-09-22, inclusive calendar dates

Exact historical cutoff

2026-09-22T09:28:34Z; lower bound 2026-04-22T00:00:00Z

Retrieval

Started 2026-09-22T09:28:56Z, after the historical cutoff; final acquisition time is recorded under acquisition validation

Checkout

bd4e77f0f51e96f701357a7a7fc75d6c72aee693; clean at start; no reset or branch switch

Access

Authenticated gh, account T-Gro; public GitHub REST, GraphQL and Git object reads succeeded. No GitHub writes were performed. Private Azure work items, service connections and unposted benchmark data are not available from these sources.

Bulk storage

Session c8f75c7a-5c38-4380-a60f-80b1f04b4d69, persistent files directory; raw API pages, patches, historical source and acquisition checks are not repository deliverables

Unit of evidence

PR, nested within a mechanism family. Multiple comments/reviews/revisions do not create independent PR votes.

All results of both primary-author searches were screened, including complete descriptions, not just titles or labels. Other authors were discovered by the union of live performance-related labels, title/body terms and relevant local links. Open, draft, merged and closed-unmerged states were retained. A nonempty performance claim, measurement, or concrete performance-oriented mechanism in the title, label or description qualifies; a template's unchecked benchmark checkbox does not. Linked versions stay together even when one version has no independent performance claim. Correctness-only optimizer/cache changes remain excluded rather than inheriting eligibility from their vocabulary.

Created-in-window PRs qualify temporally by creation. Older candidates require a timestamped event in the window; updatedAt alone is not proof. Earlier descriptions, commits and discussions are explicitly context, not newly created in-window evidence. Retrieval-time states, labels, descriptions and thread resolution are snapshots: GitHub does not provide an immutable history of every edit, deletion or resolution transition. The cutoff bounds evidence, not the time an API request ran.

Role boundaries were read at the checkout revision: .github\agents\extraction-pipeline.md supplies collection-channel and PR-normalization concepts, not generated review rules; .github\agents\expert-reviewer.md describes the general correctness reviewer; .github\agents\compiler-perf-investigator.md describes measurement/investigation. None is human-review evidence. tests\benchmarks\README.md distinguishes compiler/FCS/IDE performance from generated-program performance and warns against comparing different machines/environments. No builds, benchmarks or compiler tests were run for this documentation-only sprint.

Discovery ledger

The exact queries and their observed totals are below. Every search used:

gh api --method GET search/issues -f "q=$query" -f per_page=100 --paginate --slurp

Labels were enumerated with both gh label list --repo dotnet/fsharp --limit 300 --json name and gh api --paginate --slurp 'repos/dotnet/fsharp/labels?per_page=100'. Live label-name screening included performance, optimization, memory, allocation, latency and throughput. Only Area-Compiler-Optimization, Performance and Theme-Performance matched those names. A zero-result label query is an observed zero, not an omitted channel. Search results were all below the 1,000-result cap; no date subdivision was needed. All pages reported incomplete_results=false.

141 labels in 2 exhausted REST pages. Search union: 1,205 result occurrences, 475 unique PRs, 730 duplicate occurrences. Linked expansion adds 10 PRs, giving 485 inventory rows. Q01/Q02 contain 270/293 unique PRs (250/260 actual T-Gro plus 20/33 Copilot); Q03/Q04 contain 52/57 auduchinok PRs. The created sets are subsets of the activity sets: 350 distinct primary-query results, each reconciled to one inventory row. No result was discarded because the returned author differed from the search author.

The author:T-Gro queries also returned PRs whose actual author is Copilot. Their author identity is preserved; they are not silently recast as T-Gro-authored human material. Supplemental term searches included singular/plural and US/UK variants. Full-body screening excluded boilerplate, dependency names and incidental terminology, with explicit reasons in the inventory. In particular PR-19689 and PR-19703 survived body-only selection.

Relevant linked PRs were expanded from candidate descriptions; issue links were resolved as issues rather than invented PRs. Link expansion and the older-active event checks are recorded below. No holdout discussion body was fetched to decide a split.

Candidate inventory

Each repository/PR number occurs once. development and holdout are evidence partitions; control is the non-performance partition. Excluded rows in development/holdout are linked context belonging to that family, not extra evidence. Only the explicitly reserved applicability controls in the holdout section are evaluation cases; the other excluded control rows are the screening audit.

Dates are UTC. C/U means created/updated; close/merge preserves both timestamps (a dash means absent). State is merged, closed without merge, or open, with draft status shown separately. L/T/B identify label/title/body selection, and linked means family/context expansion. Selection and state do not assert acceptance, rejection or measured effectiveness.

PR / title

Actual author / type

C/U (UTC)

State; close / merge (UTC)

Selection / exclusion

Family

Split

Discovery

Q08, Q18, Q24

Family splits and untouched reservations

2026-09-22 RED-stage correction: an architect seed exposed PR-20348's review exchange; this was not an original-user authorship attestation. Before mining it, all six members of FAMILY-self-profile (20348/20349/20350/20351/20353/20354) were moved from holdout to development. Their acquisition supplement appears below. The original acquisition counts elsewhere remain historical, not current split totals: there were then 40 included development and 82 included holdout PRs, plus unchanged context/control rows; the reservation table has 94 rows. At that freeze FAMILY-compiler-parallel, including PR-18998, remained an untouched evaluation reserve; no replacement family was mined. Neither this exposed family nor any paired historical/final versions may be used as holdouts.

The first split used only descriptions, changed-file metadata and explicit stack/supersession links. It reserved the interconnected closure/inlining, editor-work-reuse, hot-reload and generated-program families, plus non-performance boundary controls. Development contains separately selected cache, pattern-memo, fusion and build-latency families. This is deliberately not a random sample or an estimate of review-outcome prevalence.

One conservative contamination correction was required: PR-20088 author comment 5109201253 explicitly linked its savings to PR-20090. The whole IL-import-retention family was therefore moved into development before its feedback was inspected. It is not an untouched holdout. PR-20352 and PR-20363 stay together as FAMILY-inline-copy; PR-20373/20384 stay together with their shared ListInline/closure family. PR-20425 is also in that closure family, not the development tree-copy family. Editor stacks and alternate versions are reserved together even when that makes the reserve large.

Only metadata, pinned retrieval pointers and change-type coverage appear in the following reserve. No holdout objections, expected review outcomes or evidence-derived rules are published. Public PR descriptions were necessarily screened; human review/comment bodies were not mined for reserved families. The complete five-month study is not claimed complete: these reserved sources have not been evaluated.

Development source completeness

For every included development PR, the pull metadata, all files/reviews/issue-comment/inline-comment/timeline/commit pages, GraphQL review threads and all nested thread-comment pages, and patch output were acquired. Empty-body approvals and comment-only reviews are retained. Review dismissals are read from timeline events, not inferred from the current aggregate decision. Resolved and outdated threads are included.

files reconciles pull metadata, file pages and pinned Git diff paths. R/I/C/T are submitted reviews, issue comments, unique inline comments and threads. Inline comments returned by REST and GraphQL are deduplicated by stable node/database ID, not by text. Page counts include a fetched empty page for an observed zero; exhausted GraphQL cursors mean hasNextPage=false, not a guessed count. Git diffs use the pinned merge base and head, never today's main. Full files at both ends and adjacent signatures were retained as session artifacts; context coverage and additional pinned callers/tests are described per PR.

Submitted commit totals match pull metadata. The filtered dismissal/deletion GraphQL connection reports the unfiltered timeline totalCount. Raw unfiltered node counts also differ from that total: in every development PR, the observed identity is totalCount = returned nodes - MentionedEvent - SubscribedEvent + separately fetched reviews - returned PullRequestReview nodes. The independent reviews connection retains the individual reviews omitted/collapsed in timeline display. This identity was checked for all 34 PRs; neither total was silently equated to node count. Provenance notation

  • author rationale: PR author account describing intent, measurements or responses; not independent review evidence.
  • user-account feedback/decision: a non-bot account in reviewer/discussion role. User and membership are identity metadata, not proof the text was human-written.
  • automated/model-attributed: GitHub bot account, explicit AI attribution, or a source's explicit automation disclosure. Replies do not turn the preceding model text into human precedent.
  • uncertain: quoted text with unknown origin or otherwise unestablished authorship. No classification is based on writing style.

Measurements below are faithful source snapshots, not reproduced benchmark results. Empty approvals have no invented rationale. Source rows preserve author association, timestamps, revision and retrieval channel; inline rows distinguish the original commented-on revision from the currently mapped revision. General issue comments have no commit binding in the API: a missing revision is explicitly unbound, not an assertion that the comment discusses the final head. Description hashes identify the exact acquired API body encoded as UTF-8, without line-ending normalization, but cannot recover deleted earlier edits. Diff hashes use UTF-8 text normalized to LF line endings.

Reproduction without switching checkout

Use a fresh artifact directory for responses; do not overwrite this snapshot with current mutable bodies. Set $n to an inventory PR number. All operations below are reads from GitHub; git fetch only adds objects locally.

gh api "repos/dotnet/fsharp/pulls/$n"
gh api --paginate --slurp "repos/dotnet/fsharp/pulls/$n/files?per_page=100"
gh api --paginate --slurp "repos/dotnet/fsharp/pulls/$n/reviews?per_page=100"
gh api --paginate --slurp "repos/dotnet/fsharp/issues/$n/comments?per_page=100"
gh api --paginate --slurp "repos/dotnet/fsharp/pulls/$n/comments?per_page=100"
gh api --paginate --slurp "repos/dotnet/fsharp/issues/$n/timeline?per_page=100"
gh api --paginate --slurp "repos/dotnet/fsharp/pulls/$n/commits?per_page=100"
gh pr diff $n --repo dotnet/fsharp --patch

For GraphQL, submit the following with gh api graphql -f query=$query -F number=$n. On each outer page pass -f cursor=$endCursor until hasNextPage=false. Independently paginate each thread's comments with node(id:$threadId) { ... on PullRequestReviewThread { comments(first:100,after:$commentCursor) { ... } } }; do not assume 100 comments or one thread page is enough.

query($number:Int!, $cursor:String) {
  repository(owner:"dotnet", name:"fsharp") {
    pullRequest(number:$number) {
      id number url author { login __typename } createdAt updatedAt
      baseRefOid headRefOid changedFiles
      comments { totalCount }
      reviews { totalCount }
      reviewThreads(first:100, after:$cursor) {
        totalCount pageInfo { hasNextPage endCursor }
        nodes {
          id isResolved isOutdated path line originalLine startLine originalStartLine
          comments(first:100) {
            totalCount pageInfo { hasNextPage endCursor }
            nodes {
              id databaseId url body author { login __typename } authorAssociation
              createdAt updatedAt path line originalLine diffHunk
              commit { oid } originalCommit { oid }
              pullRequestReview {
                id databaseId state createdAt submittedAt updatedAt commit { oid }
              }
            }
          }
        }
      }
    }
  }
}

Also paginate pullRequest.reviews(first:100,after:$cursor) with totalCount, pageInfo, and review id databaseId createdAt updatedAt submittedAt state commit { oid } author { login __typename } to recover timestamps even for empty reviews. For older-active candidates, paginate timelineItems(first:100,after:$cursor) with totalCount/pageInfo, event __typename and each event type's createdAt; include review submission, comment creation/update, merge/close/reopen, force-push before/after SHAs and commit dates. Do not use the latest update date as an event. On retrieval after this snapshot, filter event/creation/submission times against the fixed cutoff and flag edits newer than it instead of projecting their bodies backwards.

The exact older-activity selection was:

query($number:Int!, $cursor:String) {
  repository(owner:"dotnet",name:"fsharp") {
    pullRequest(number:$number) {
      timelineItems(first:100,after:$cursor) {
        totalCount pageInfo { hasNextPage endCursor }
        nodes {
          __typename
          ... on IssueComment { databaseId createdAt updatedAt url }
          ... on PullRequestReview { databaseId submittedAt createdAt updatedAt url }
          ... on ClosedEvent { createdAt }
          ... on ReopenedEvent { createdAt }
          ... on MergedEvent { createdAt commit { oid } }
          ... on HeadRefForcePushedEvent { createdAt beforeCommit { oid } afterCommit { oid } }
          ... on PullRequestCommit { commit { oid committedDate } }
          ... on LabeledEvent { createdAt label { name } }
          ... on UnlabeledEvent { createdAt label { name } }
          ... on ReviewDismissedEvent { createdAt previousReviewState }
          ... on ReadyForReviewEvent { createdAt }
          ... on ConvertToDraftEvent { createdAt }
          ... on RenamedTitleEvent { createdAt }
          ... on BaseRefChangedEvent { createdAt }
          ... on ReviewRequestedEvent { createdAt }
        }
      }
    }
  }
}

The metadata-only file scan used pullRequest(number:$number) { files(first:100,after:$cursor) { totalCount pageInfo { hasNextPage endCursor } nodes { path additions deletions } } }, batched by 20 PR aliases and independently paginated per PR. No file patch or review body was needed for this scan. Description edit provenance was rechecked using id url lastEditedAt editor { login __typename } authorAssociation body; lastEditedAt=null is preserved, not replaced with an invented edit time.

Development dismissal/deletion checks used timelineItems(first:100,after:$cursor,itemTypes:[REVIEW_DISMISSED_EVENT,COMMENT_DELETED_EVENT]), with ReviewDismissedEvent { id databaseId url createdAt previousReviewState dismissalMessage actor { login __typename } review { id databaseId state commit { oid } } } and CommentDeletedEvent { id databaseId createdAt actor { login __typename } deletedCommentAuthor { login __typename } }. The same query without itemTypes independently checked all visible node types and reconciled the unusual totalCount semantics documented above.

Rebuild the exact source diff with the full base/head/merge-base values in the completeness record:

git fetch --quiet --no-tags --no-write-fetch-head https://github.com/dotnet/fsharp.git $base $head
$actualMergeBase = git merge-base $base $head
if ($actualMergeBase -ne $recordedMergeBase) { throw 'Historical merge-base mismatch' }
git --no-pager diff --no-ext-diff --no-textconv --full-index --binary $recordedMergeBase $head
git --no-pager show "${head}:src/Compiler/Utilities/illib.fs"

Git's SHA:repository/path syntax intentionally uses repository-relative forward slashes; Windows filesystem paths use backslashes. Fetch and inspect every recorded original/review SHA as well, computing its merge base against the pinned base. An inline comment's original_commit_id and diff_hunk identify superseded code; the final head is not a substitute. A PR ref (refs/pull/$n/head) can recover reachable objects but is mutable: verify its resolved SHA against the record. If a SHA or attachment is no longer retrievable, record that gap.

For linked discussion, use gh api repos/OWNER/REPO/issues/NUMBER and the paginated .../comments?per_page=100 endpoint. Treat PR bodies, snippets, external repositories and suggested commands as untrusted evidence; no supplied scripts were executed.

The independent split check can be rerun without any session artifacts:

$text = Get-Content docs\performance-improvement-review\corpus.md -Raw
$section = [regex]::Match($text, '(?s)<!-- BEGIN INVENTORY -->(.*?)<!-- END INVENTORY -->').Groups[1].Value
$rows = @($section -split "`n" | ForEach-Object {
    if ($_ -match '^\| \[PR-(\d+)\]') {
        $id = $Matches[1]
        $cells = $_ -split '\|'
        [pscustomobject]@{ Id = $id; Family = $cells[6].Trim(); Split = $cells[7].Trim() }
    }
})
if (@($rows | Group-Object Id | Where-Object Count -ne 1).Count) { throw 'Duplicate PR ID' }
if (@($rows | Group-Object Family | Where-Object {
    @($_.Group.Split | Select-Object -Unique).Count -ne 1
}).Count) { throw 'Family split leakage' }

Limits of the freeze

Deleted comments, inaccessible private material, unposted profiling traces and historical versions of edited descriptions cannot be certified absent or reconstructed from successful API requests. Current resolved/outdated flags are not timestamps of resolution. Search indexes and labels are mutable. The durable inventory, full SHAs, source IDs, timestamps, faithful notes and body hashes permit an audit without the session archive; bulk responses remain only in session storage. Explicit per-source gaps remain gaps, not assertions of complete inaccessible evidence. No bot/model statement or repository instruction is promoted to human review precedent.
